class="infobox-below"><a href="/wiki/Pope_Alexander_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="Pope Alexander (disambiguation)">Other popes  named Alexander</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pope Alexander VII</b> (<a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Alessandro VII</i>; 13 February 1599&#160;&#8211;&#32;22 May 1667), born <b>Fabio Chigi</b>, was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> and ruler of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> from 7 April 1655 to his death in May 1667.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>He began his career as a vice-<a href="/wiki/Papal_legate" title="Papal legate">papal legate</a>, and he held various diplomatic positions in the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a>. He was ordained as a priest in 1634, and he became <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Nardo" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Nardo">bishop of Nardo</a> in 1635. He was later transferred in 1652, and he became <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Imola" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Imola">bishop of Imola</a>. <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_X" title="Pope Innocent X">Pope Innocent X</a> made him <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">secretary of state</a> in 1651, and in 1652, he was appointed a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">cardinal</a>.
</p><p>Early in his papacy, Alexander, who was seen as an anti-<a href="/wiki/Nepotist" class="mw-redirect" title="Nepotist">nepotist</a> at the time of his election, lived simply; later, however, he gave jobs to his relatives, who eventually took over his administration. His administration worked to support the <a href="/wiki/Jesuits" title="Jesuits">Jesuits</a>. However, his administration's relations with <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> were strained due to his frictions with French diplomats.
</p><p>Alexander was interested in architecture and supported various urban projects in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>. He also wrote poetry and patronized artists who expanded the decoration of churches. His theological writings included discussions of <a href="/wiki/Heliocentrism" title="Heliocentrism">heliocentrism</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Biography">Biography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Biography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Born in <a href="/wiki/Siena" title="Siena">Siena</a>, a member of the illustrious banking family of <a href="/wiki/Chigi-Albani_family" class="mw-redirect" title="Chigi-Albani family">Chigi</a> and a great-nephew of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_V" title="Pope Paul V">Pope Paul V</a> (1605–1621),<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Fabio Chigi was privately tutored and eventually received doctorates of philosophy, law, and theology from the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Siena" title="University of Siena">University of Siena</a>.
</p><p>Fabio's elder brother, Mario, married Berenice, the daughter of Tiberio della Ciala, producing four children, of whom two survived: Agnes and Flavio. Flavio (1631–1693) was created cardinal by his uncle on 9 April 1657. His brother, Augusto Chigi (1595–1651), married Olimpia della Ciaia (1614–1640) and continued the family line as the parents of Agostino Chigi, Prince Farnese. Fabio's sister Onorata Mignanelli married Firmano Bichi; their son Antonio was named Bishop of <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Montalcino" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Montalcino">Montalcino</a> (1652–1656) and then of <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Osimo" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Osimo">Osimo</a> (1656–1659), and was named a cardinal (<a href="/wiki/In_pectore" title="In pectore">in pectore</a>) by his uncle, Alexander VII, on 9 April 1657. The appointment was made public on 10 November 1659.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Another of his nephews was Giovanni Bichi, whom he appointed Admiral of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_Navy" title="Papal Navy">Papal Navy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Papal_diplomat">Papal diplomat</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Papal diplomat">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1627 he began his apprenticeship as vice-<a href="/wiki/Papal_legate" title="Papal legate">papal legate</a> at <a href="/wiki/Ferrara" title="Ferrara">Ferrara</a>, and on recommendations from two cardinals he was appointed <a href="/wiki/Inquisitor" title="Inquisitor">Inquisitor</a> of <a href="/wiki/Malta" title="Malta">Malta</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Chigi was ordained a priest in Rome in December 1634. He was appointed <i>Referendarius utriusque signaturae</i>, which made him a prelate and gave him the right to practice before the Roman courts. On 8 January 1635, Chigi was named <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Nard%C3%B2" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Nardò">Bishop of Nardò</a> in southern Italy and consecrated on 1 July 1635<sup id="cite_ref-Gauchat_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gauchat-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> by <a href="/wiki/Miguel_Juan_Balaguer_Camarasa" class="mw-redirect" title="Miguel Juan Balaguer Camarasa">Miguel Juan Balaguer Camarasa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Malta" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Malta">Bishop of Malta</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CathHierFabChi_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CathHierFabChi-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>  On 13 May 1652 he was transferred to the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Imola" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Imola">Bishopric of Imola</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Gauchat_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gauchat-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Bishop Chigi was named <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nuncio_to_Cologne" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Nuncio to Cologne">nuncio in Cologne</a> (1639–1651) on 11 June 1639.  There, he supported <a href="/wiki/Urban_VIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Urban VIII">Urban VIII</a>'s condemnation of the heretical book <i><a href="/wiki/Augustinus_(Jansenist_book)" title="Augustinus (Jansenist book)">Augustinus</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Cornelius_Jansen" title="Cornelius Jansen">Cornelius Jansen</a>, Bishop of Ypres, in the papal Bull <i>In eminenti</i> of 1642.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Though expected to take part in the negotiations which led in 1648 to the <a href="/wiki/Peace_of_Westphalia" title="Peace of Westphalia">Peace of Westphalia</a>, Bishop Chigi (and other Catholic delegates) declined to deliberate with persons whom the Catholic Church considered <a href="/wiki/Christian_heresy" class="mw-redirect" title="Christian heresy">heretics</a>. Negotiations therefore took place in two cities, <a href="/wiki/Osnabr%C3%BCck" title="Osnabrück">Osnabrück</a> and <a href="/wiki/M%C3%BCnster" title="Münster">Münster</a> in <a href="/wiki/Westphalia" title="Westphalia">Westphalia</a>, with intermediaries travelling back and forth between the Protestant and the Catholic delegates.  Chigi, of course, protested on behalf of the Papacy, when the treaties were finally completed, against the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Westphalia" class="mw-redirect" title="Treaty of Westphalia">Treaty of Westphalia</a> once the instruments were finally completed.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>  Pope Innocent himself stated that the Peace  "is null, void, invalid, unjust, damnable, reprobate, inane, empty of meaning and effect for all time."<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The Peace ended the <a href="/wiki/Thirty_Years%27_War" title="Thirty Years&#39; War">Thirty Years' War</a> (1618–1648) and established the balance of European power that lasted until the wars of the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolution" title="French Revolution">French Revolution</a> (1789).
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Secretary_of_State_and_Cardinal">Secretary of State and Cardinal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Secretary of State and Cardinal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_X" title="Pope Innocent X">Pope Innocent X</a> (1644–1655) recalled Chigi to Rome.  In December 1651 Pope Innocent named Cardinal Chigi <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Secretary of State</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> He was created cardinal by Innocent X in the Consistory of 19 February 1652, and on 12 March was granted the title of <a href="/wiki/Cardinal-Priest" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal-Priest">Cardinal-Priest</a> of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_del_Popolo" title="Santa Maria del Popolo">Santa Maria del Popolo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Papacy">Papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Election_as_pope">Election as pope</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Election as pope">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Alexander VII</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:C_o_a_Alessandro_VII.svg" class="image"><img alt="C o a Alessandro VII.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/C_o_a_Alessandro_VII.svg/60px-C_o_a_Alessandro_VII.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="57" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/C_o_a_Alessandro_VII.svg/90px-C_o_a_Alessandro_VII.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/C_o_a_Alessandro_VII.svg/120px-C_o_a_Alessandro_VII.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1383" data-file-height="1319" /></a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Sanct(issim)e Pater</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data">None</td></tr></tbody></table>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_1655" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, 1655">Papal conclave, 1655</a></div>
<p>When Innocent X died on 1 January 1655, Cardinal Chigi was elected pope after eighty days in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_election" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal election">conclave</a>, on 7 April 1655, taking the name of Alexander VII, in honor of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Alexander_III" title="Pope Alexander III">Pope Alexander III</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On the morning of his election as he went to celebrate Mass before the final ballot, Chigi was greeted by his friend Luigi Omodei who, knowing that Chigi was soon to be elected, said: "At length that day has come, so desired by me, and so happy for the Church!" Chigi replied to Omodei by reciting Virgil and said: "That day I shall always recollect with grief; with reverence also for the gods so willed it". During the final ballot, Chigi had cast his vote for Giulio Cesare Sacchetti while in the accesso casting it for Giovanni Maria Battista Pallotta. Upon his election, one of the cardinals remarked: "What a singular thing! The Spaniards disinterestedly wished you to be pope; the French wished it, though they had at first excluded you; the young men chose a man already aged, and the Barberini a man who was not their own creature!"<sup id="cite_ref-UC_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UC-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Upon his election, he was crowned on 18 April 1655 by the Cardinal Protodeacon Gian Giacomo Teodoro Trivulzio before taking possession of the Basilica of San Giovanni Laterano on the following 9 May.
</p><p>One of his first acts as pope was to order <a href="/wiki/Olimpia_Maidalchini" title="Olimpia Maidalchini">Olimpia Maidalchini</a> (known popularly as "la papessa" due to her extraordinary influence with Pope Innocent X) to return to her native town of Orvieto.<sup id="cite_ref-EC_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EC-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Maidalchini had been a rival of sorts since Chigi's office as Secretary of State commanded much power which Maidalchini hoped to utilize for her own ends while she worked with Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Decio_Azzolino" title="Decio Azzolino">Decio Azzolino</a> to try to circumvent Chigi's authority with the hopes of having Chigi either effectively sidelined or replaced.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nepotism">Nepotism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Nepotism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The conclave believed he was strongly opposed to the <a href="/wiki/Nepotism" title="Nepotism">nepotism</a> that had been a feature of previous popes. In the first year of his reign, Alexander VII lived simply and forbade his relations even to visit Rome, but in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_consistory" title="Papal consistory">consistory</a> of 24 April 1656, Pope Alexander announced that his brother and nephews would be coming to assist him in Rome. His nephew, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Flavio_Chigi_(1631%E2%80%931693)" title="Flavio Chigi (1631–1693)">Flavio Chigi</a> assumed the position of <a href="/wiki/Cardinal-nephew" title="Cardinal-nephew">cardinal-nephew</a>. The administration was given largely into the hands of his relatives,<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> and nepotism became entrenched as it had been in the Baroque Papacy. Cardinal Flavio began work on the Villa Chigi-Versaglia at Formello in 1664.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When announcing to the cardinals in 1656 that he would summon his relatives to Rome, the pontiff asked that each cardinal provide their opinion on his suggestion. The cardinals agreed, but attached several conditions to ensure that nepotism did not run too rampant. The pope eventually received his relatives in an audience, however, the meeting was formal and his relatives were required to kneel for the entirety of the audience as the pope informed them of why they had been summoned and what was expected of them.<sup id="cite_ref-UC_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UC-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Urban_and_architectural_projects_in_Rome">Urban and architectural projects in Rome</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Urban and architectural projects in Rome">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:237px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Anselm_van_Hulle_-_Portrait_of_the_Papal_Nuntius_Fabio_Chigi.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Anselm_van_Hulle_-_Portrait_of_the_Papal_Nuntius_Fabio_Chigi.jpg/235px-Anselm_van_Hulle_-_Portrait_of_the_Papal_Nuntius_Fabio_Chigi.jpg" decoding="async" width="235" height="291" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Anselm_van_Hulle_-_Portrait_of_the_Papal_Nuntius_Fabio_Chigi.jpg/353px-Anselm_van_Hulle_-_Portrait_of_the_Papal_Nuntius_Fabio_Chigi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Anselm_van_Hulle_-_Portrait_of_the_Papal_Nuntius_Fabio_Chigi.jpg/470px-Anselm_van_Hulle_-_Portrait_of_the_Papal_Nuntius_Fabio_Chigi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="802" data-file-height="994" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Anselm_van_Hulle_-_Portrait_of_the_Papal_Nuntius_Fabio_Chigi.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Fabio Chigi as Papal Nuntius to the Peace of Westphalia negotiations, by <a href="/wiki/Anselm_van_Hulle" title="Anselm van Hulle">Anselm van Hulle</a> (c. 1646)</div></div></div><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:242px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Guidi_Alexander_VII.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Guidi_Alexander_VII.jpg/240px-Guidi_Alexander_VII.jpg" decoding="async" width="240" height="317" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Guidi_Alexander_VII.jpg/360px-Guidi_Alexander_VII.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Guidi_Alexander_VII.jpg/480px-Guidi_Alexander_VII.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2387" data-file-height="3149" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Guidi_Alexander_VII.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Alexander VII, by Domenico Guidi</div></div></div>
<p>A number of pontiffs are renowned for their urban planning in the city of Rome—for example, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Julius_II" title="Pope Julius II">Pope Julius II</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Sixtus_V" title="Pope Sixtus V">Pope Sixtus V</a>—but Alexander VII's numerous urban interventions were not only diverse in scope and scale but demonstrated a consistent planning and architectural vision that the glorification and embellishment of the city, ancient and modern, sacred and secular, should be governed by order and decorum.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Central to Alexander's urbanism was the idea of <i>teatro</i> or urban theatre<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> whereby his urban interventions became the grand settings or showpieces appropriate to the dignity of Rome and the Head of the Catholic Church. Therefore, and although the scales are vastly different, the small <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_della_Pace" title="Santa Maria della Pace">Santa Maria della Pace</a> and its piazza are as much a teatro as the imposing monumental colonnade that forms <a href="/wiki/Piazza_San_Pietro" class="mw-redirect" title="Piazza San Pietro">Piazza San Pietro</a> in front of <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a>.
</p><p>The various urban and architectural projects carried out during Alexander's reign were recorded in engravings by <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Battista_Falda" title="Giovanni Battista Falda">Giovanni Battista Falda</a> and the first volume was published in 1665. The volumes were published by <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Giacomo_de_Rossi" title="Giovanni Giacomo de Rossi">Giovanni Giacomo de Rossi</a> under the title <i>Il Nuovo Teatro delle fabriche et edificij in prospettiva di Roma moderna sotto il felice pontificato di N.S. Alessandro VII.</i><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>  A rival publication documenting these projects was published by Rossi's cousin Giovanni Battista de Rossi who employed the young <a href="/wiki/Southern_Netherlands" title="Southern Netherlands">Flemish</a> architectural draughtsman <a href="/wiki/Lieven_Cruyl" class="mw-redirect" title="Lieven Cruyl">Lieven Cruyl</a> to produce drawings of Rome, 10 of which were published in 1666 under the title <i>Prospectus Locorum Urbis Romae Insignium</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His preferred architect was the sculptor and architect <a href="/wiki/Gianlorenzo_Bernini" class="mw-redirect" title="Gianlorenzo Bernini">Gianlorenzo Bernini</a><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> but he also gave architectural commissions to the painter and architect <a href="/wiki/Pietro_da_Cortona" title="Pietro da Cortona">Pietro da Cortona</a>. Of the three leading architects of the Roman High <a href="/wiki/Baroque" title="Baroque">Baroque</a>, only <a href="/wiki/Francesco_Borromini" title="Francesco Borromini">Francesco Borromini</a> fared not so well under Alexander; this may be because he thought Borromini's architectural forms willful but also Borromini could be notoriously difficult. Nonetheless, Alexander's family heraldic emblems of the mons or mountains with stars and oak leaves, adorn Borromini's<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> church of <a href="/wiki/Sant%27Ivo_alla_Sapienza" title="Sant&#39;Ivo alla Sapienza">Sant'Ivo alla Sapienza</a> and many other works of his reign.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexander took a keen personal interest in his urban and architectural projects and made notes of these in his diaries.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> His projects in Rome included: the church and piazza at <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_della_Pace" title="Santa Maria della Pace">Santa Maria della Pace</a>; the <a href="/wiki/Via_del_Corso" title="Via del Corso">Via del Corso</a>, <a href="/wiki/Piazza_Colonna" title="Piazza Colonna">Piazza Colonna</a> and associated buildings; reworking of the <a href="/wiki/Porta_del_Popolo" title="Porta del Popolo">Porta del Popolo</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Piazza_del_Popolo" title="Piazza del Popolo">Piazza del Popolo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_del_Popolo" title="Santa Maria del Popolo">Santa Maria del Popolo</a>; <a href="/wiki/Piazza_San_Pietro" class="mw-redirect" title="Piazza San Pietro">Piazza San Pietro</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/Scala_Regia_(Vatican)" title="Scala Regia (Vatican)">Scala Regia</a> and interior embellishments in the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Palace" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican Palace">Vatican Palace</a> and <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's</a>; <a href="/wiki/Sant%27Andrea_al_Quirinale" title="Sant&#39;Andrea al Quirinale">Sant'Andrea al Quirinale</a>; part of the <a href="/wiki/Palazzo_del_Quirinale" class="mw-redirect" title="Palazzo del Quirinale">Palazzo del Quirinale</a>; the <a href="/wiki/Arsenal_of_Civitavecchia" title="Arsenal of Civitavecchia">arsenal</a> at <a href="/wiki/Civitavecchia" title="Civitavecchia">Civitavecchia</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> the obelisk and elephant in <a href="/wiki/Piazza_della_Minerva" title="Piazza della Minerva">Piazza della Minerva</a>; and the Palazzo Chigi.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>  The Palazzo Chigi in Rome is not to be confused with the Palazzo Chigi in S. Quirico d'Orcia in Tuscany,<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>  or the Palazzo Chigi di Formello.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Andrea_Sacchi_Pope_Alexander_VII_Drawing.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Andrea_Sacchi_Pope_Alexander_VII_Drawing.jpg/220px-Andrea_Sacchi_Pope_Alexander_VII_Drawing.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Andrea_Sacchi_Pope_Alexander_VII_Drawing.jpg/330px-Andrea_Sacchi_Pope_Alexander_VII_Drawing.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Andrea_Sacchi_Pope_Alexander_VII_Drawing.jpg/440px-Andrea_Sacchi_Pope_Alexander_VII_Drawing.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3299" data-file-height="3305" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Andrea_Sacchi_Pope_Alexander_VII_Drawing.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Drawing of Pope Alexander VII by <a href="/wiki/Andrea_Sacchi" title="Andrea Sacchi">Andrea Sacchi</a></div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Malta">Malta</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Malta">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Before being elected as Pontiff, Chigi served as Inquisitor on the Island of Malta where he resided mostly at the <a href="/wiki/Inquisitor%27s_Palace" title="Inquisitor&#39;s Palace">Inquisitor's Palace</a> in Birgu (alias Città Vittoriosa). At that time Malta was a fiefdom of the Knights Hospitallers of the Sovereign Order of Saint John of Jerusalem, Knights of Malta, from which he purchased one hundred slaves in 1662 for his naval squadron. <sup id="cite_ref-nih_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nih-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sweden">Sweden</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Sweden">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Expand_section plainlinks metadata ambox mbox-small-left ambox-content" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg" class="image"><img alt="[icon]" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/20px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png" decoding="async" width="20" height="14" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/30px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/40px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="44" data-file-height="31" /></a></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs expansion</b>. You can help by <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=">adding to it</a>.  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">September 2016</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The conversion of Queen <a href="/wiki/Christina_of_Sweden" class="mw-redirect" title="Christina of Sweden">Christina of Sweden</a> (1632–1654) occurred during Alexander VII's reign. After her abdication the queen came to reside in Rome, where she was confirmed in her <a href="/wiki/Baptism" title="Baptism">baptism</a> by the Pope, in whom she found a generous friend and benefactor, on <a href="/wiki/Christmas_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="Christmas Day">Christmas Day</a>, 1655. She was described by the Pope as 'a queen without a realm, a Christian without faith, and a woman without shame.'<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> He was also said to have accused her of being 'a woman born of a barbarian, barbarously brought up and living with barbarous thoughts', therefore indicating that their relationship may have been contentious at best.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Shortly after her arrival in Rome, she quickly became the center of Roman fashion and parties. However, following the pre-Lenten Carnival in 1656, Alexander VII quickly regretted having ever invited her to Rome since there existed an atmosphere of immorality which was linked to the Carnival. While the pontiff had originally hoped that Christina would become an inspiration for those considering conversion to the faith, he was dismayed that her interests were primarily political, even to the point that she helped plot the conquest of Naples with <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Mazarin" title="Cardinal Mazarin">Cardinal Jules Mazarin</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="France">France</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: France">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In foreign policy his instincts were not as <a href="/wiki/Humanism" title="Humanism">humanist</a> or as successful. Alexander VII's pontificate was shadowed by continual friction with <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Mazarin" title="Cardinal Mazarin">Cardinal Mazarin</a>, adviser to <a href="/wiki/Louis_XIV_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis XIV of France">Louis XIV of France</a> (1643–1715), who had opposed him during the negotiations that led to the Peace of Westphalia and who defended the prerogatives of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_France" title="Catholic Church in France">Gallican Church</a>. During the conclave, he had been hostile to Chigi's election, but was in the end compelled to accept him as a compromise. However, he prevented Louis XIV from sending the usual embassy of obedience to Alexander VII, and, while he lived, he foiled the appointment of a French ambassador to Rome, diplomatic affairs being meantime conducted by cardinal protectors, generally personal enemies of the Pope. In 1662, the equally hostile <a href="/wiki/Duc_de_Crequi" class="mw-redirect" title="Duc de Crequi">Duc de Crequi</a> was made ambassador. By his abuse of the traditional right of asylum granted to ambassadorial precincts in Rome, he precipitated a quarrel between France and the papacy, which resulted in Alexander VII's temporary loss of <a href="/wiki/Avignon" title="Avignon">Avignon</a> and his forced acceptance of the humiliating <a href="/w/index.php?title=Treaty_of_Pisa&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Treaty of Pisa (page does not exist)">treaty of Pisa</a> in 1664.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Spain_and_Portugal">Spain and Portugal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Spain and Portugal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Expand_section plainlinks metadata ambox mbox-small-left ambox-content" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg" class="image"><img alt="[icon]" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/20px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png" decoding="async" width="20" height="14" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/30px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/40px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="44" data-file-height="31" /></a></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs expansion</b>. You can help by <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=">adding to it</a>.  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">September 2016</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Alexander VII favored the <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spanish</a> in their claims against <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>, which had reestablished its traditional independence in 1640. His pontificate was also marked by protracted controversies with Portugal.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Jesuits_and_Jansenism">Jesuits and Jansenism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Jesuits and Jansenism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Formulary_controversy" title="Formulary controversy">Formulary controversy</a></div>
<p>Alexander VII favoured the <a href="/wiki/Jesuits" title="Jesuits">Jesuits</a>. When the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Venetians</a> called for help in <a href="/wiki/Crete" title="Crete">Crete</a> against the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Turks" title="Ottoman Turks">Ottoman Turks</a>, the Pope extracted in return a promise that the Jesuits should be permitted back in Venetian territory, from which they had been expelled in 1606. He also continued to take the Jesuit part in their conflict with the <a href="/wiki/Jansenism" title="Jansenism">Jansenists</a>, whose condemnation he had vigorously supported as advisor to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_X" title="Pope Innocent X">Pope Innocent X</a>. The French Jansenists professed that the propositions condemned in 1653 were not in fact to be found in <i>Augustinus</i>, written by <a href="/wiki/Cornelius_Jansen" title="Cornelius Jansen">Cornelius Jansen</a>. Alexander VII confirmed that they were too, by the bull <i>Ad Sanctam Beati Petri Sedem</i> (16 October 1656) declaring that five propositions extracted by a group of theologians from the <a href="/wiki/Coll%C3%A8ge_de_Sorbonne" class="mw-redirect" title="Collège de Sorbonne">Sorbonne</a> out of Jansen's work, mostly concerning <a href="/wiki/Divine_grace" title="Divine grace">grace</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Fall_(religion)" class="mw-redirect" title="Fall (religion)">fallen nature of man</a>, were <a href="/wiki/Christian_heresy" class="mw-redirect" title="Christian heresy">heretical</a>, including the proposition according to which to say "that <a href="/wiki/Christ" class="mw-redirect" title="Christ">Christ</a> died, or shed His blood for all men" would be a <a href="/wiki/Semipelagianism" class="mw-redirect" title="Semipelagianism">semipelagian</a> error. He also sent to France his famous "<a href="/wiki/Formulary_controversy" title="Formulary controversy">formulary</a>", that was to be signed by all the clergy as a means of detecting and extirpating Jansenism and which inflamed public opinion, leading to <a href="/wiki/Blaise_Pascal" title="Blaise Pascal">Blaise Pascal</a>'s defense of Jansenism.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Works">Works</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Works">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Alexander VII disliked the business of state, preferring <a href="/wiki/Literature" title="Literature">literature</a> and <a href="/wiki/Philosophy" title="Philosophy">philosophy</a>; a collection of his <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a> poems appeared at Paris in 1656 under the title <i>Philomathi Labores Juveniles</i>. He also encouraged architecture, and the general improvement of Rome, where houses were razed to straighten and widen streets and where he had the opportunity to be a great patron for <a href="/wiki/Gian_Lorenzo_Bernini" title="Gian Lorenzo Bernini">Gian Lorenzo Bernini</a>: the decorations of the church of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_del_Popolo" title="Santa Maria del Popolo">Santa Maria del Popolo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Titular_church" title="Titular church">titular churches</a> for several of the Chigi cardinals, the <a href="/wiki/Scala_Regia" title="Scala Regia">Scala Regia</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Chair_of_St._Peter" class="mw-redirect" title="Chair of St. Peter">Chair of St. Peter</a> in the Vatican Basilica. In particular, he sponsored Bernini's construction of the beautiful colonnade in the <a href="/wiki/Piazza" class="mw-redirect" title="Piazza">piazza</a> of <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a>.
</p><p>According to Rev. William Roberts, Alexander VII wrote one of the most authoritative documents related to the <a href="/wiki/Heliocentrism" title="Heliocentrism">heliocentrism</a> issue. However, such document is not about any astronomic model and is not part of the Magisterium Ecclesiae.  The Pope published his <i>Index Librorum Prohibitorum Alexandri VII Pontificis Maximi jussu editus</i> which presented anew the contents of the <a href="/wiki/Index_of_Forbidden_Books" class="mw-redirect" title="Index of Forbidden Books">Index of Forbidden Books</a> which had condemned many works related to many different matters: among them were the works of Copernicus and Galileo. He prefaced this with the <a href="/wiki/Papal_bull" title="Papal bull">bull</a> <i>Speculatores Domus Israel</i> (1592), stating his reasons: "in order that the whole history of each case may be known." 'For this purpose,'  the Pontiff stated, 'we have caused the Tridentine and Clementine Indices to be added to this general Index, and also all the relevant decrees up to the present time, that have been issued since the Index of our predecessor Clement, that nothing profitable to the faithful interested in such matters might seem omitted."<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Among those included were the previous decrees placing various heliocentric works on the Index ("...which we will should be considered as though it were inserted in these presents, together with all, and singular, the things contained therein...") and, according to Roberts, using his Apostolic authority he bound the faithful to its contents ("...and approve with Apostolic authority by the tenor of these presents, and: command and enjoin all persons everywhere to yield this Index a constant and complete obedience...")<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>  Thus, for the geocentrists, Alexander turned definitively against the heliocentric view of the solar system.  After Alexander VII's pontificate, the <i>Index</i> underwent a number of revisions.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> "In 1758 the general prohibition against works advocating heliocentrism was removed from the Index of prohibited books, although the specific ban on uncensored versions of the <i>Dialogue</i> and Copernicus's <i>De Revolutionibus</i> remained. All traces of official opposition to heliocentrism by the church disappeared in 1835 when these works were finally dropped from the Index".<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The Index was abolished entirely in 1966.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Theology">Theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Alexander VII's <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Constitution" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Constitution">Apostolic Constitution</a>, <i>Sollicitudo omnium ecclesiarum</i> (8 December 1661),<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> laid out the doctrine of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a> of the Blessed Virgin Mary in terms almost identical to those utilized by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a> when he issued his infallible definition <a href="/wiki/Ineffabilis_Deus" title="Ineffabilis Deus">Ineffabilis Deus</a>. Pius IX cites Alexander VII's bull in his footnote 11.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Morality">Morality</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Morality">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 18 March 1666 in a decree issued, Alexander VII issued a correction on various moral matters that expanded upon a decree he had issued previously on 24 September 1665. In his decree, Alexander VII confirmed the authority and rulings from the Council of Trent while advising on matters ranging from sacramental confession, heresy, to proper courtship practices. The pontiff also confirmed the rulings made by predecessors such as Urban VIII in matters that concerned moral teachings.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_XI" title="Pope Innocent XI">Pope Innocent XI</a> later expanded upon some of his points in 1679.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Ecclesial_activities">Ecclesial activities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Ecclesial activities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Consistories">Consistories</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Consistories">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Alexander_VII" title="Cardinals created by Alexander VII">Cardinals created by Alexander VII</a></div>
<p>Alexander VII created 38 cardinals in six consistories which included <a href="/wiki/Flavio_Chigi_(1631%E2%80%931693)" title="Flavio Chigi (1631–1693)">Flavio Chigi</a>, his nephew in the role of <a href="/wiki/Cardinal-Nephew" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal-Nephew">Cardinal-Nephew</a>, while naming <a href="/wiki/Pope_Clement_IX" title="Pope Clement IX">Giulio Rospigliosi</a> as a cardinal and whom would eventually succeed him as Pope Clement IX. Out of all the cardinals whom he had named, the pope had reserved five of those names <i><a href="/wiki/In_pectore" title="In pectore">in pectore</a></i> and announced them at later dates.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religious_orders">Religious orders</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Religious orders">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Alexander VII reformed the Constitution for the <a href="/wiki/Cistercians" title="Cistercians">Cistercians</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_brief" title="Papal brief">papal brief</a> <i>In suprema</i> on 19 April 1666 which effectively ended a dispute that had lasted for decades on the question of reform which had long since divided the Cistercians. The pope also played a role in changes made to the <a href="/wiki/Barnabites" title="Barnabites">Barnabites</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Piarists" title="Piarists">Piarists</a> (1656). In 1666, he formally approved the Congregation of Jesus and Mary which had been founded by <a href="/wiki/John_Eudes" title="John Eudes">Saint John Eudes</a> while in 1655 he had given his approval to the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_the_Mission" title="Congregation of the Mission">Congregation of the Mission</a> which had been founded by <a href="/wiki/Vincent_de_Paul" title="Vincent de Paul">Saint Vincent de Paul</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Liturgical_actions">Liturgical actions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Liturgical actions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1661, the pope forbade the translation of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Missal" title="Roman Missal">Roman Missal</a> into <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> which had been published in 1660 by the priest Joseph de Voisin. With the French episcopacy condemning the bilingual Missal in late 1660, the pontiff ratified this in a papal bull on 12 January 1661.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> In 1656, he revoked the decree of Innocent X and allowed the Chinese rites to be used by the Jesuit missionaries in China. Alexander VII expanded upon this in 1659 when he dispensed the Chinese clergy from having to pray the Divine Office in Latin.<sup id="cite_ref-EC_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EC-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonizations_and_beatifications">Canonizations and beatifications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Canonizations and beatifications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Throughout his pontificate, Alexander VII beatified two individuals (<a href="/wiki/Francis_de_Sales" title="Francis de Sales">Francis de Sales</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pedro_de_Arbu%C3%A9s" title="Pedro de Arbués">Pedro de Arbués</a>) and canonized five others which included <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_III_of_Castile" title="Ferdinand III of Castile">Ferdinand III</a> and <a href="/wiki/Felix_of_Valois" title="Felix of Valois">Félix de Valois</a> amongst others.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Eastern_Churches">Eastern Churches</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Eastern Churches">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1661, the pope assured the <a href="/wiki/Chaldean_Catholic_Church" title="Chaldean Catholic Church">Chaldean</a> <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Babylon" class="mw-redirect" title="Patriarch of Babylon">Patriarch</a> <a href="/wiki/Shimun_XII_Yoalaha" title="Shimun XII Yoalaha">Shimun XII Yoalaha</a> that the Latin Rite episcopacy would support those of the Chaldean Rite. During his papacy, the pope also dispatched a mission consisting of <a href="/wiki/Carmelite" class="mw-redirect" title="Carmelite">Carmelite</a> friars to <a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a> in order to evangelize and spread the faith in the <a href="/wiki/Middle_East" title="Middle East">Middle East</a>. As a show of support for the <a href="/wiki/Syriac_Christianity" title="Syriac Christianity">Syriac Rite</a>, the pope also created the <a href="/wiki/Syriac_Catholic_Archeparchy_of_Aleppo" title="Syriac Catholic Archeparchy of Aleppo">Archdiocese of Aleppo</a> in 1659.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tombstone_of_Pope_Alexander_VII_St._Peter%27s_Basilica_Vatican.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Tombstone_of_Pope_Alexander_VII_St._Peter%27s_Basilica_Vatican.jpg/250px-Tombstone_of_Pope_Alexander_VII_St._Peter%27s_Basilica_Vatican.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="375" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Tombstone_of_Pope_Alexander_VII_St._Peter%27s_Basilica_Vatican.jpg/375px-Tombstone_of_Pope_Alexander_VII_St._Peter%27s_Basilica_Vatican.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Tombstone_of_Pope_Alexander_VII_St._Peter%27s_Basilica_Vatican.jpg/500px-Tombstone_of_Pope_Alexander_VII_St._Peter%27s_Basilica_Vatican.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="5472" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tombstone_of_Pope_Alexander_VII_St._Peter%27s_Basilica_Vatican.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Tomb_of_Pope_Alexander_VII" title="Tomb of Pope Alexander VII">tomb of Pope Alexander VII</a>, by Gianlorenzo Bernini</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Last_moments_and_death">Last moments and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Last moments and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tomb_of_Pope_Alexander_VII" title="Tomb of Pope Alexander VII">Tomb of Pope Alexander VII</a></div>
<p>Alexander VII died at age 68 from kidney failure and whose health initially began to fail around March, with his ailment consistently causing him great pain. He kept his coffin in his bedroom, and a skull (carved by famed sculptor <a href="/wiki/Gian_Lorenzo_Bernini" title="Gian Lorenzo Bernini">Bernini</a>) on his writing table, because he was always aware that he would someday die. Suddenly taken by a fever on 18 May, he died on 22 May. A seventeenth-century pamphlet credited to <a href="/wiki/Philip_Ayres_(poet)" title="Philip Ayres (poet)">Ayres</a>, titled <i>A short Account of the Life and Death of Pope Alexander VII</i>, contains many fascinating details about Alexander's passing. According to this pamphlet, Alexander, although bedridden, wanted to celebrate the Passion to ready himself for his impending death. Neither his surgeon nor his confessor was able to persuade him to save his strength. He blessed the large crowd of people on <a href="/wiki/Easter_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Easter day">Easter</a>, the last time they would ever see him alive.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A precise and detailed account of the pope's last days is given in the <i>Diary</i> of the principal Master of Ceremonies, Msgr. Fulvio Servantio, an official eyewitness to all the proceedings.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<table>

<tbody><tr>
<th>A short Account of the Life and Death of Pope Alexander VII<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</th></tr>
<tr>
<td><blockquote><p>Promoted to the cardinalship eight of those select person, whom he supposed, for their great worth and labours, bestowed for the good of the Papal See, had merited the advancement to so high dignity.<br />
</p><p>But his disease increasing, four days after, he was assaulted by a grievous fit, from which he concluded that his sickness was mortal, and not withstanding, it grew more and more violent daily, yet for all this, he had thoughts of performing the long ceremonies of Holy Thursday, to prepare himself for death, as he said, by meditating on the sacred mysteries of the passion o Jesus Christ, and would have executed his intentions in despight of his pains, if his physicians and chyrurgeons, together with his confessor had not perswaded him to the contrary; remonstrating to him the inconveniencies which might arise, from the hard labours which are inseparable from such prolixe ceremonies. And although he was perswaded by them all that time, yet was he resolved with that little strength he had left him (though much broken and extenuated by his disease) on Easter-day upon the Gallery of Monte Cavallo, where this function is used to be performed, with a solemn benediction in Pontificalthus, to bless the people, which there flocked in exceeding great multitudes, being driven there-unto not only out of devotion, but also by a desire of seeing their pastor yet whole and alive. He blessed them, having raised himself up twice according to the custom, without the help of the pontifical seat; and this was the lat time that he saw his flock, or they him. <br />
Ex lastly, be recommends to their care and protection, his Cardinal Nephew, his aged brother, and the rest of his kindred, and himself to their prayers. This being spoken, he lifted up his hands and blessed them, and then their eminencies approached to the bed side with tears in their eyes, and after they had taken pains to comfort him, with great tenderness they kissed his hand, and departed.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> <br /> 
At last, they being all departed, and only his familiar friends and ghostly-fathers continuing in the room with him, he altogether applied himself to his devotion, often repeating these words, Cupio disolvi et esse cum Christo ["I desire to be discharged from my debt and to be with Christ"]. And those which assisted him he caused continually to read spiritual books, &amp; divers prayers, and psalms, especially the penitential psalms, &amp;c. <br />
After he had received both the Eucharist and the Extreme Unction, he disposed himself for his Transit, with a marvelous undantedness;  and had already even lost his Speech, when one of his religious men standing by exhorted him to do an act of contrition, and to aske God pardon of his sins, he collecting his breath, which was flying away, with a most lanquisting voice, which coud hardly be understood, answered Ita ["Yes"]. The same added that he should hope in the mercies of God, who is always ready to shower down his mercies upon a penitent heart; the pope answered with the same weakness of speech, Certe ["Certainly"]. Which were the last words which proceeded out of his mouth. <br />
He was often visited by the cardinals, contrary to the former customs, who were willing to be round about his bed till his end. And on Sunday the 22. of May, about 22. of the clock, he quietly rendred his spirits to his Creator, in the 60 year of his age, and 13 of his pontificate; and the same evening, the usual ceremony being performed by the cardinal lord Chamberlain, the corps were arrayed in the accustomed vestments, put into a litter of crimson velvet open on all sides, compassed round about by the penitentiary fathers, with lighted torches in their hands, accompanied by the guards and light horses, followed by the artillery, and with the Rexe Guard of Curiassiers, being carried to the Vatican, and there the next morning opened, there was found on one part of the lungs, fastned to one of his sides, a touch of a black spot; one of his kidneys wasted, and some carnosities of fleshy kyrnels instead of it, from whence the passage of the urine was hindred; and an ulcer of the reins, which of all his other diseases was the worst: from thence, being embalmed and pontifically appareled, he was carried the next day to the cathedral of St. Peter, and placed in the chapel of the most Holy Sacrament, where was a concourse of an infinite number of people, to kiss his feet, and take from him whatsoever they could lay hands on, to preserve to themselves as holy reliques. Finis
</p>
</blockquote>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Alexander VII died in 1667 and was memorialised in a <a href="/wiki/Tomb_of_Pope_Alexander_VII" title="Tomb of Pope Alexander VII">spectacular tomb</a> by Bernini, however, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_XI" title="Pope Innocent XI">Pope Innocent XI</a> ordered that the naked Truth be covered up with a drapery colored in white.<sup id="cite_ref-UC_18-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UC-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> It is famous for the skeleton holding a gilded hourglass, just above the doors. He was succeeded by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Clement_IX" title="Pope Clement IX">Pope Clement IX</a> (1667–69). As the pope lay dying, he said to the cardinals gathered at his bedside: "We never aspired to the tiara, nor took any steps to reach it. We have employed the moneys of the apostolical chamber solely in the service of the Catholic religion, and the embellishment of Rome and the building of churches. We were a whole year pontiff before we summoned any relative of ours to Rome, and we only at length did so because the Sacred College vanquished our unwillingness. We exhort you to elect a successor qualified to repair any errors we have committed in our pontificate".<sup id="cite_ref-UC_18-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UC-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Memory">Memory</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Alexander_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Memory">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The poet John Flowre wrote a poem about the tomb of Pope Alexander (in 1667).<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<li id="cite_note-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-2">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Note on numbering: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Alexander_V" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope Alexander V">Pope Alexander V</a> is now considered an anti-pope. At the time however, this fact was not recognised and so the fifth true Pope Alexander took the official number VI. This caused the true sixth Pope Alexander to take the number VII. This has advanced the numbering of all subsequent Popes Alexander by one. Popes Alexander VI-VIII are really the fifth through seventh popes by that name.</span>
